=== SOURCE CODE (java) ===
package org.example;

import java.net.URLDecoder;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

public class UrlPath {
    private List<String> segments;
    private boolean withEndTag;

    public UrlPath() {
        this.segments = new ArrayList<>();
        this.withEndTag = false;
    }

    public void add(String segment) {
        this.segments.add(fixPath(segment));
    }

    public void parse(String path, String charset) {
        if (path != null && !path.isEmpty()) {
            if (path.endsWith("/")) {
                this.withEndTag = true;
            }

            path = fixPath(path);
            if (path != null && !path.isEmpty()) {
                String[] split = path.split("/");
                for (String seg : split) {
                    try {
                        String decodedSeg = URLDecoder.decode(seg, charset);
                        this.segments.add(decodedSeg);
                    } catch (Exception e) {
                        e.printStackTrace();
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    public static String fixPath(String path) {
        if (path == null || path.isEmpty()) {
            return "";
        }

        String segmentStr = path.strip().replaceAll("^/+|/+$", "");
        return segmentStr;
    }

    public List<String> getSegments() {
        return segments;
    }

    public boolean isWithEndTag() {
        return withEndTag;
    }
}

=== PROMPT ===
You are an expert programmer. Translate the following java code to py.
- Keep behavior identical, including inputs/outputs, side effects, edge cases, and exceptions.
- Use idiomatic py only when it does not change behavior.

```java
package org.example;

import java.net.URLDecoder;
import java.util.ArrayList;
import java.util.List;

public class UrlPath {
    private List<String> segments;
    private boolean withEndTag;

    public UrlPath() {
        this.segments = new ArrayList<>();
        this.withEndTag = false;
    }

    public void add(String segment) {
        this.segments.add(fixPath(segment));
    }

    public void parse(String path, String charset) {
        if (path != null && !path.isEmpty()) {
            if (path.endsWith("/")) {
                this.withEndTag = true;
            }

            path = fixPath(path);
            if (path != null && !path.isEmpty()) {
                String[] split = path.split("/");
                for (String seg : split) {
                    try {
                        String decodedSeg = URLDecoder.decode(seg, charset);
                        this.segments.add(decodedSeg);
                    } catch (Exception e) {
                        e.printStackTrace();
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    public static String fixPath(String path) {
        if (path == null || path.isEmpty()) {
            return "";
        }

        String segmentStr = path.strip().replaceAll("^/+|/+$", "");
        return segmentStr;
    }

    public List<String> getSegments() {
        return segments;
    }

    public boolean isWithEndTag() {
        return withEndTag;
    }
}
```

Use a short stable mapping path that preserves behavior.

Keep the reasoning compact and single-pass:
- Identify only the pieces that must remain equivalent: signatures, I/O contract, stateful behavior, control flow, and key API semantics.
- Turn that mapping into target-language structure promptly.
- Use one short trap scan for the single most likely language-pair failure mode.
- If a mismatch appears, patch only the affected local section and recheck once.
- If no concrete mismatch appears, close instead of reopening the mapping repeatedly.

Output only the translated py code in a code block.

    bytes_out = bytearray()
    i = 0
    n = len(s)
    while i < n:
        c = s[i]
        if c == '+':
            bytes_out.append(0x20)
            i += 1
        elif c == '%':
            if i + 2 >= n:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Incomplete trailing escape (%) pattern")

Implementation of `_java_chars(s)`:
```
def _utf16_code_units(s):
    units = []
    for ch in s:
        cp = ord(ch)
        if cp >= 0x10000:
            cp -= 0x10000
            units.append(0xD800 + (cp >> 10))
            units.append(0xDC00 + (cp & 0x3FF))
        else:
            units.append(cp)
    return units
```

```
import unicodedata
def _is_java_whitespace(ch):
    cp = ord(ch)
    if cp in (0x00A0, 0x2007, 0x202F):
        return False
    if cp in (0x0009, 0x000A, 0x000B, 0x000C, 0x000D) or 0x001C <= cp <= 0x001F:
        return True
    return unicodedata.category(ch) in ('Zs', 'Zl', 'Zp')
```

```
start = 0
end = len(s)
while start < end and _is_java_whitespace(s[start]): start += 1
while end > start and _is_java_whitespace(s[end-1]): end -= 1
return s[start:end]
```

Let's write code:

```python
import re
import traceback
import unicodedata


def _is_java_whitespace(ch):
    cp = ord(ch)
    if cp in (0x00A0, 0x2007, 0x202F):
        return False
    if cp in (0x0009, 0x000A, 0x000B, 0x000C, 0x000D) or 0x001C <= cp <= 0x001F:
        return True
    return unicodedata.category(ch) in ('Zs', 'Zl', 'Zp')


def _java_strip(s):
    start = 0
    end = len(s)
    while start < end and _is_java_whitespace(s[start]):
        start += 1
    while end > start and _is_java_whitespace(s[end - 1]):
        end -= 1
    return s[start:end]


def _utf16_code_units(s):
    units = []
    for ch in s:
        cp = ord(ch)
        if cp >= 0x10000:
            cp -= 0x10000
            units.append(0xD800 + (cp >> 10))
            units.append(0xDC00 + (cp & 0x3FF))
        else:
            units.append(cp)
    return units


def _url_decode(s, charset):
    units = _utf16_code_units(s)
    out = bytearray()
    i = 0
    n = len(units)
    while i < n:
        u = units[i]
        if u == 0x2B:  # '+'
            out.append(0x20)
            i += 1
        elif u == 0x25:  # '%'
            if i + 2 >= n:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Incomplete trailing escape (%) pattern")
            hex_str = chr(units[i + 1]) + chr(units[i + 2])
            try:
                v = int(hex_str, 16)
            except ValueError:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Invalid hex digits in escape (%) pattern") from None
            out.append(v & 0xFF)
            i += 3
        else:
            out.append(u & 0xFF)
            i += 1
    return bytes(out).decode(charset, errors='replace')


class UrlPath:
    def __init__(self):
        self.segments = []
        self.withEndTag = False

    def add(self, segment):
        self.segments.append(self.fixPath(segment))

    def parse(self, path, charset):
        if path is not None and path != "":
            if path.endswith("/"):
                self.withEndTag = True

            path = self.fixPath(path)
            if path is not None and path != "":
                for seg in path.split("/"):
                    try:
                        decoded_seg = _url_decode(seg, charset)
                        self.segments.append(decoded_seg)
                    except Exception:
                        traceback.print_exc()

    @staticmethod
    def fixPath(path):
        if path is None or path == "":
            return ""
        segment_str = re.sub(r'^/+|/+$', '', _java_strip(path))
        return segment_str

    def getSegments(self):
        return self.segments

    def isWithEndTag(self):
        return self.withEndTag
```

Need to check `_url_decode` hex_str construction: `chr(units[i+1]) + chr(units[i+2])`. If units are ASCII hex digits, this works. If units are non-ASCII, `chr` gives the character, and `int` may fail. Java's `substring` would also produce a string of those chars, and `Integer.parseInt` would fail. Good.

But note: In Java, `s.substring(i+1, i+3)` returns the original chars, which may be surrogate pairs if the two chars are not ASCII? But `%` escape should be followed by hex digits, which are ASCII. If not, invalid. Our method is fine.

One nuance: In `_url_decode`, when we encounter a `%`, we consume the next two code units. But if the next two code units are a surrogate pair representing a supplementary character, `chr(units[i+1]) + chr(units[i+2])` would produce a string of two surrogate code points? Actually `chr(0xD83D)` returns a lone surrogate character, which is invalid in Python string? In Python, you can have lone surrogates in strings? Yes, e.g., `chr(0xD83D)` returns a string with a surrogate code point. `int` on that would fail. Java's `substring` would also produce a String with those two chars, and `Integer.parseInt` would fail. Good.

Now, let's review the code for any syntax errors.

- `import re`, `import traceback`, `import unicodedata` at top.

- `_java_strip(s)`: assumes `s` is str. Good.
- `_utf16_code_units(s)`: iterates over `s`. For each char, if cp >= 0x10000, append surrogate pair. Good.
- `_url_decode(s, charset)`: uses `_utf16_code_units`. `hex_str = chr(units[i + 1]) + chr(units[i + 2])`. If units are ASCII, fine. If units are not ASCII, `int` may fail. Good.
- In `_url_decode`, `raise ValueError(...) from None` to avoid chaining. Java's exception doesn't have a cause. Good.

We need to implement strict hex parsing like Java. We can avoid `int` and manually parse two hex digits. Or validate that both chars are hex digits using a regex or `all(c in '0123456789abcdefABCDEF' for c in hex_str)`. Java's `Integer.parseInt` accepts only optional sign and hex digits, no whitespace. We can implement a strict hex parser:
```
def _parse_hex_byte(hex_str):
    if len(hex_str) != 2:
        raise ValueError
    # allow optional sign? Actually substring is exactly 2 chars. If first char is '+' or '-', second must be hex digit.
    # Java's Integer.parseInt("+1", 16) works, but "+1" length 2. If first char is '+', second '1' -> 1.
    # If first char is '-', second '1' -> -1.
    # If first char is hex digit and second is hex digit, normal.
    # If first char is '+' or '-', second must be hex digit. But what about "+-" -> invalid.
    # We can implement by checking if the string matches: optional sign followed by one or more hex digits? But length exactly 2.
    # Actually parseInt requires at least one digit. So "+1" valid, "1" valid, "-1" valid, "++" invalid, "+ " invalid.
    # Since length is 2, possible forms:
    # - two hex digits: "1a"
    # - sign + hex digit: "+1", "-1"
    # - hex digit + sign? "1+" invalid because parseInt("1+", 16) throws.
    # So we can check:
    # if hex_str[0] in '+-':
    #     if hex_str[1] not in hex_digits: raise
    #     v = int(hex_str, 16)
    # else:
    #     if any(c not in hex_digits for c in hex_str): raise
    #     v = int(hex_str, 16)
```
But Python's `int("+1", 16)` works, and `int("-1", 16)` works. For `"1+"`, Python `int("1+", 16)` raises ValueError. For `" 1"`, Python `int(" 1", 16)` returns 1, so we need to reject whitespace. We can simply check that `hex_str` matches `^[+-]?[0-9a-fA-F]$`? Wait, length 2, so optional sign plus one hex digit, or two hex digits. Actually `^[+-]?[0-9a-fA-F]$` would match "+1" and "1" but not "1a" (two hex digits). Need `^([0-9a-fA-F]{2}|[+-][0-9a-fA-F])$`. That covers "+1", "-1", "1a". But what about "1" (length 1) not possible. So:
```

    raise ValueError
v = int(hex_str, 16)
```
But wait, Java's `Integer.parseInt("+a", 16)` returns 10. `Integer.parseInt("-a", 16)` returns -10. So `[+-][0-9a-fA-F]` works. What about `++1` length 3 not possible. Good.

Alternatively, we can manually compute:
```
def _parse_hex_byte(hex_str):
    if len(hex_str) != 2:
        raise ValueError
    sign = 1
    i = 0
    if hex_str[0] == '+':
        i = 1
    elif hex_str[0] == '-':
        sign = -1
        i = 1
    if i >= len(hex_str):
        raise ValueError
    digit = _hex_val(hex_str[i])
    if digit < 0:
        raise ValueError
    # Wait, if sign present, there is only one digit. If no sign, there are two digits.
    # But if sign present and there is a second digit? e.g., "+1a" length 3 not possible.
    # For no sign, we need two digits. So:
    if i == 0:
        # two digits
        d0 = _hex_val(hex_str[0])
        d1 = _hex_val(hex_str[1])
        if d0 < 0 or d1 < 0: raise ValueError
        v = d0 * 16 + d1
    else:
        # one digit
        d = _hex_val(hex_str[1])
        if d < 0: raise ValueError
        v = d
    return sign * v
```
But Java's `Integer.parseInt("+1", 16)` returns 1, not 1? Yes. `Integer.parseInt("-1", 16)` returns -1. So sign applies to the whole value. For "+1", value 1. For "-1", value -1. For "+a", value 10. For "-a", value -10. So our manual parse should handle.

However, is it possible for the two-character substring to be "+1" and Java parse it as 1? Yes. But wait, URLDecoder expects percent-encoded bytes. A percent escape is `%XX` where X is a hex digit. Java's URLDecoder uses `Integer.parseInt(s.substring(i+1, i+3), 16)`, which is lenient and accepts `+` or `-` as a sign. So `%+1` is actually decoded as byte 0x01 by Java. That's a quirk. We should preserve it.

But is `%+1` a valid URL encoding? No, but Java's URLDecoder accepts it. So we should too.

Now, what about `%1+`? Java `Integer.parseInt("1+", 16)` throws. Our regex would reject. Good.

What about `% 1`? Java throws. Our regex rejects. Good.

What about `%1 `? Java throws. Our regex rejects. Good.

What about `%1a`? Java parses 0x1a. Our regex matches `[0-9a-fA-F]{2}`. Good.

What about `%+a`? Java parses 10. Our regex matches `[+-][0-9a-fA-F]`. Good.

What about `%-1`? Java parses -1, byte 0xFF. Our regex matches, v=-1, append v & 0xFF = 255. Good.

So we should implement strict parsing with regex or manual. Let's update `_url_decode` to use a helper `_parse_hex_byte` that matches Java's `Integer.parseInt` behavior for length 2 strings.

But wait: Java's `Integer.parseInt(String s, int radix)` accepts a string that may have length > 1 with sign and digits. For length 2, it can be sign + digit or two digits. It also accepts "+1" and "-1". It does NOT accept "1+" or "+-". It also does NOT accept whitespace. So regex `^([0-9a-fA-F]{2}|[+-][0-9a-fA-F])$` is correct. But what about "++"? Regex doesn't match. Good.

Let's implement `_parse_hex_byte` using regex:
```
_HEX_BYTE_RE = re.compile(r'^([0-9a-fA-F]{2}|[+-][0-9a-fA-F])$')

def _parse_hex_byte(hex_str):
    if not _HEX_BYTE_RE.match(hex_str):
        raise ValueError
    return int(hex_str, 16)
```
But `int("+a", 16)` returns 10. `int("-a", 16)` returns -10. Good. `int("1a", 16)` returns 26. Good.

However, `int("+1", 16)` returns 1. Good.

Let's write the final code:

```python
import re
import traceback
import unicodedata

_HEX_BYTE_RE = re.compile(r'^([0-9a-fA-F]{2}|[+-][0-9a-fA-F])$')


def _parse_hex_byte(hex_str):
    if not _HEX_BYTE_RE.match(hex_str):
        raise ValueError
    return int(hex_str, 16)


def _is_java_whitespace(ch):
    cp = ord(ch)
    if cp in (0x00A0, 0x2007, 0x202F):
        return False
    if cp in (0x0009, 0x000A, 0x000B, 0x000C, 0x000D) or 0x001C <= cp <= 0x001F:
        return True
    return unicodedata.category(ch) in ('Zs', 'Zl', 'Zp')


def _java_strip(s):
    start = 0
    end = len(s)
    while start < end and _is_java_whitespace(s[start]):
        start += 1
    while end > start and _is_java_whitespace(s[end - 1]):
        end -= 1
    return s[start:end]


def _utf16_code_units(s):
    units = []
    for ch in s:
        cp = ord(ch)
        if cp >= 0x10000:
            cp -= 0x10000
            units.append(0xD800 + (cp >> 10))
            units.append(0xDC00 + (cp & 0x3FF))
        else:
            units.append(cp)
    return units


def _url_decode(s, charset):
    units = _utf16_code_units(s)
    out = bytearray()
    i = 0
    n = len(units)
    while i < n:
        u = units[i]
        if u == 0x2B:  # '+'
            out.append(0x20)
            i += 1
        elif u == 0x25:  # '%'
            if i + 2 >= n:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Incomplete trailing escape (%) pattern")
            hex_str = chr(units[i + 1]) + chr(units[i + 2])
            try:
                v = _parse_hex_byte(hex_str)
            except ValueError:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Invalid hex digits in escape (%) pattern") from None
            out.append(v & 0xFF)
            i += 3
        else:
            out.append(u & 0xFF)
            i += 1
    return bytes(out).decode(charset, errors='replace')


class UrlPath:
    def __init__(self):
        self.segments = []
        self.withEndTag = False

    def add(self, segment):
        self.segments.append(self.fixPath(segment))

    def parse(self, path, charset):
        if path is not None and path != "":
            if path.endswith("/"):
                self.withEndTag = True

            path = self.fixPath(path)
            if path is not None and path != "":
                for seg in path.split("/"):
                    try:
                        decoded_seg = _url_decode(seg, charset)
                        self.segments.append(decoded_seg)
                    except Exception:
                        traceback.print_exc()

    @staticmethod
    def fixPath(path):
        if path is None or path == "":
            return ""
        segment_str = re.sub(r'^/+|/+$', '', _java_strip(path))
        return segment_str

    def getSegments(self):
        return self.segments

    def isWithEndTag(self):
        return self.withEndTag
```

But `$` with `^` and no `re.MULTILINE` matches end of string. It's fine. However, `$` also matches before a trailing newline, so `"1\n"` would match `[0-9a-fA-F]{2}`? Actually `"1\n"` length 2? ```

```

Final code:

```python
import re
import traceback
import unicodedata

_HEX_BYTE_RE = re.compile(r'(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{2}|[+-][0-9a-fA-F])')


def _parse_hex_byte(hex_str):
    if not _HEX_BYTE_RE.fullmatch(hex_str):
        raise ValueError
    return int(hex_str, 16)


def _is_java_whitespace(ch):
    cp = ord(ch)
    if cp in (0x00A0, 0x2007, 0x202F):
        return False
    if cp in (0x0009, 0x000A, 0x000B, 0x000C, 0x000D) or 0x001C <= cp <= 0x001F:
        return True
    return unicodedata.category(ch) in ('Zs', 'Zl', 'Zp')


def _java_strip(s):
    start = 0
    end = len(s)
    while start < end and _is_java_whitespace(s[start]):
        start += 1
    while end > start and _is_java_whitespace(s[end - 1]):
        end -= 1
    return s[start:end]


def _utf16_code_units(s):
    units = []
    for ch in s:
        cp = ord(ch)
        if cp >= 0x10000:
            cp -= 0x10000
            units.append(0xD800 + (cp >> 10))
            units.append(0xDC00 + (cp & 0x3FF))
        else:
            units.append(cp)
    return units


def _url_decode(s, charset):
    units = _utf16_code_units(s)
    out = bytearray()
    i = 0
    n = len(units)
    while i < n:
        u = units[i]
        if u == 0x2B:  # '+'
            out.append(0x20)
            i += 1
        elif u == 0x25:  # '%'
            if i + 2 >= n:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Incomplete trailing escape (%) pattern")
            hex_str = chr(units[i + 1]) + chr(units[i + 2])
            try:
                v = _parse_hex_byte(hex_str)
            except ValueError:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Invalid hex digits in escape (%) pattern") from None
            out.append(v & 0xFF)
            i += 3
        else:
            out.append(u & 0xFF)
            i += 1
    return out.decode(charset, errors='replace')


class UrlPath:
    def __init__(self):
        self._segments = []
        self._withEndTag = False

    def add(self, segment):
        self._segments.append(self.fixPath(segment))

    def parse(self, path, charset):
        if path is not None and path != "":
            if path.endswith("/"):
                self._withEndTag = True

            path = self.fixPath(path)
            if path is not None and path != "":
                for seg in path.split("/"):
                    try:
                        decoded_seg = _url_decode(seg, charset)
                        self._segments.append(decoded_seg)
                    except Exception:
                        traceback.print_exc()

    @staticmethod
    def fixPath(path):
        if path is None or path == "":
            return ""
        segment_str = re.sub(r'^/+|/+$', '', _java_strip(path))
        return segment_str

    def getSegments(self):
        return self._segments

    def isWithEndTag(self):
        return self._withEndTag
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
>```python
import re
import traceback
import unicodedata

_HEX_BYTE_RE = re.compile(r'(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{2}|[+-][0-9a-fA-F])')


def _parse_hex_byte(hex_str):
    if not _HEX_BYTE_RE.fullmatch(hex_str):
        raise ValueError
    return int(hex_str, 16)


def _is_java_whitespace(ch):
    cp = ord(ch)
    if cp in (0x00A0, 0x2007, 0x202F):
        return False
    if cp in (0x0009, 0x000A, 0x000B, 0x000C, 0x000D) or 0x001C <= cp <= 0x001F:
        return True
    return unicodedata.category(ch) in ('Zs', 'Zl', 'Zp')


def _java_strip(s):
    start = 0
    end = len(s)
    while start < end and _is_java_whitespace(s[start]):
        start += 1
    while end > start and _is_java_whitespace(s[end - 1]):
        end -= 1
    return s[start:end]


def _utf16_code_units(s):
    units = []
    for ch in s:
        cp = ord(ch)
        if cp >= 0x10000:
            cp -= 0x10000
            units.append(0xD800 + (cp >> 10))
            units.append(0xDC00 + (cp & 0x3FF))
        else:
            units.append(cp)
    return units


def _url_decode(s, charset):
    units = _utf16_code_units(s)
    out = bytearray()
    i = 0
    n = len(units)
    while i < n:
        u = units[i]
        if u == 0x2B:  # '+'
            out.append(0x20)
            i += 1
        elif u == 0x25:  # '%'
            if i + 2 >= n:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Incomplete trailing escape (%) pattern")
            hex_str = chr(units[i + 1]) + chr(units[i + 2])
            try:
                v = _parse_hex_byte(hex_str)
            except ValueError:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Invalid hex digits in escape (%) pattern") from None
            out.append(v & 0xFF)
            i += 3
        else:
            out.append(u & 0xFF)
            i += 1
    return out.decode(charset, errors='replace')


class UrlPath:
    def __init__(self):
        self._segments = []
        self._withEndTag = False

    def add(self, segment):
        self._segments.append(self.fixPath(segment))

    def parse(self, path, charset):
        if path is not None and path != "":
            if path.endswith("/"):
                self._withEndTag = True

            path = self.fixPath(path)
            if path is not None and path != "":
                for seg in path.split("/"):
                    try:
                        decoded_seg = _url_decode(seg, charset)
                        self._segments.append(decoded_seg)
                    except Exception:
                        traceback.print_exc()

    @staticmethod
    def fixPath(path):
        if path is None or path == "":
            return ""
        segment_str = re.sub(r'^/+|/+$', '', _java_strip(path))
        return segment_str

    def getSegments(self):
        return self._segments

    def isWithEndTag(self):
        return self._withEndTag
```

=== TRANSLATED CODE (py) ===
import re
import traceback
import unicodedata

_HEX_BYTE_RE = re.compile(r'(?:[0-9a-fA-F]{2}|[+-][0-9a-fA-F])')


def _parse_hex_byte(hex_str):
    if not _HEX_BYTE_RE.fullmatch(hex_str):
        raise ValueError
    return int(hex_str, 16)


def _is_java_whitespace(ch):
    cp = ord(ch)
    if cp in (0x00A0, 0x2007, 0x202F):
        return False
    if cp in (0x0009, 0x000A, 0x000B, 0x000C, 0x000D) or 0x001C <= cp <= 0x001F:
        return True
    return unicodedata.category(ch) in ('Zs', 'Zl', 'Zp')


def _java_strip(s):
    start = 0
    end = len(s)
    while start < end and _is_java_whitespace(s[start]):
        start += 1
    while end > start and _is_java_whitespace(s[end - 1]):
        end -= 1
    return s[start:end]


def _utf16_code_units(s):
    units = []
    for ch in s:
        cp = ord(ch)
        if cp >= 0x10000:
            cp -= 0x10000
            units.append(0xD800 + (cp >> 10))
            units.append(0xDC00 + (cp & 0x3FF))
        else:
            units.append(cp)
    return units


def _url_decode(s, charset):
    units = _utf16_code_units(s)
    out = bytearray()
    i = 0
    n = len(units)
    while i < n:
        u = units[i]
        if u == 0x2B:  # '+'
            out.append(0x20)
            i += 1
        elif u == 0x25:  # '%'
            if i + 2 >= n:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Incomplete trailing escape (%) pattern")
            hex_str = chr(units[i + 1]) + chr(units[i + 2])
            try:
                v = _parse_hex_byte(hex_str)
            except ValueError:
                raise ValueError("URLDecoder: Invalid hex digits in escape (%) pattern") from None
            out.append(v & 0xFF)
            i += 3
        else:
            out.append(u & 0xFF)
            i += 1
    return out.decode(charset, errors='replace')


class UrlPath:
    def __init__(self):
        self._segments = []
        self._withEndTag = False

    def add(self, segment):
        self._segments.append(self.fixPath(segment))

    def parse(self, path, charset):
        if path is not None and path != "":
            if path.endswith("/"):
                self._withEndTag = True

            path = self.fixPath(path)
            if path is not None and path != "":
                for seg in path.split("/"):
                    try:
                        decoded_seg = _url_decode(seg, charset)
                        self._segments.append(decoded_seg)
                    except Exception:
                        traceback.print_exc()

    @staticmethod
    def fixPath(path):
        if path is None or path == "":
            return ""
        segment_str = re.sub(r'^/+|/+$', '', _java_strip(path))
        return segment_str

    def getSegments(self):
        return self._segments

    def isWithEndTag(self):
        return self._withEndTag
